國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國 10 月財政赤字大幅擴張、房市買氣探底，通膨壓力仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 美國 2027 財年首月財政赤字達 4,040.6 億美元，年增 42.1%。當月總收
入 3,689.4 億美元（年減 8.8%），總支出 7,730 億美元（年增 12.2%），
截至 10 月底公債總額升至 40.52 兆美元，財政壓力顯著加劇。 
⚫ 受房貸利率攀升至 7.33%影響，2026 年 10 月美國成屋銷售年率跌至 375
萬戶的 16 年新低。目前房價中位數微升至 41.67 萬美元，待售庫存增
至 159 萬戶，房市呈現「量縮、價微揚、庫存增」的態勢。考量目前就
業市場仍持續成長，一旦利率開始回落，預期將能有效釋放市場積壓的
買氣。 
⚫ 10 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%，低於上月 0.6%，惟高於市場
預期；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%持平。住房、食品及能源價格續升，其中住
房貢獻逾三成，顯示通膨壓力仍存；惟受 2025 年 10 月政府停擺影響，
10 月年增率未發布。 
⚫ 10 月美國 PPI 月增 0.3%，較上月 1.4%明顯放緩，但年增率由 6.3%上
升至 6.5%，創 2022 年 11 月以來新高。能源與服務價格漲勢降溫，惟
食品、非能源商品及貨運價格續揚，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍高，後續須
關注成本轉嫁及對 Fed 利率政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 2026 年 APEC 峰會將於 11 月 18 日至 19 日在中國深圳舉行。會前中美
相繼延長部分關稅豁免及貿易限制暫停措施至 2027 年 1 月，中國暫停
24%對美加徵關稅，惟保留 10%稅率，美國則延長 178 項中國商品關稅
豁免，釋出經貿緩和訊號，有助降低供應鏈不確定性。惟雙方結構性分
歧仍存，後續須關注川習會談成果及貿易政策走向。 
⚫ 輝達創辦人黃仁勳因婚外情及涉國安爭議引發調查，於 11 月 12 日辭去
執行長及董事職務，結束 33 年掌舵生涯，由財務長克雷斯暫代執行長。
消息重挫輝達股價及市值，並引發市場對全球 AI 產業、供應鏈及公司
治理風險的高度關注。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 10 月財政赤字顯著擴大，反映歲入下滑及政府支出負擔加重；成
屋銷售創多年低點，顯示高利率持續壓抑房市需求。儘管 CPI 及 PPI 月
增幅放緩，惟核心通膨及生產端價格壓力仍頑強，顯示物價上行風險尚

中 
國 
國際能源價格上漲抑制貿易順差擴張 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月出口年增率較 9 月續增 1.6 個百分點至 37.9%，出口總值為 4,123
億美元；進口年增率增 10.6 個百分點至 41.6%，進口總值為 3,071 億美
元。 
⚫ 10 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 9 月的 5%升至 5.7%；但消費者通膨(CPI)、
核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別回落 0.2 個百分點為 0.7%、0.8%。 
短評 
⚫ 由於國際能源價格上漲，進口增速高於出口，並抑制貿易順差擴張、減
緩外匯存底流入速度，惟此亦有助於減緩人民幣升值速度。 
⚫ 在民間消費乏力之下，企業仍大多自行吸收成本，加上政府控制零售燃
油及民生水電價格漲幅，故目前原油價格高漲僅少數反映於消費端。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.11.16 
一、 美國—10 月財政赤字大幅擴張、房市買氣探底，通膨壓力仍具韌性 
2026 年 10 月(2027 財年第 1 個月)聯邦政府財政赤字達 4,040.6 億美元，較去
年同期的 2,843.3 億美元大幅擴大 42.1% 。當月政府總收入為 3,689.4 億美元，年
減 8.8%，主因個人所得稅淨收入由去年同期的 2,169.5 億美元下滑至 1,863.8 億
美元，且 10 月關稅退稅高達 67.3 億美元(遠高於去年同期的 17.4 億美元)，大幅
抵銷了 259.5 億美元的關稅總收入，致使關稅淨收入雖較 9 月的 59.1 億美元回升
至 192.2 億美元，仍較去年同期的 313.5 億美元大減 38.7%。支出方面，10 月政
府總支出達 7,730.0 億美元，年增 12.2%，除因 11 月 1 日適逢非營業日，導致部
分現役與退役軍人薪資、退伍軍人福利及醫療保險等款項提前至 10 月撥付外，
醫療保險(1,700.5 億美元)、社會安全(1,426.9 億美元)及國防(1,288.8 億美元)等主
要項目均較去年同期成長；同時，10 月聯邦淨利息支出攀升至 1,030.8 億美元，
年增 13.4%。整體而言，在歲入下滑與福利、國防及利息支出擴張的雙重影響下，
美國新財年開局赤字顯著走高，截至 10 月底聯邦公債總額已進一步推升至 40.52
兆美元(其中公眾持有債務為 32.74 兆美元)，龐大債務規模與後續利息負擔仍將
是牽動美國財政穩健度的核心變數。 
2026 年 10 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率降至 375 萬戶，較上月減少
5.5%，較去年同期下滑 8.8%，創下自 2010 年 8 月以來的最低水準，主因是 30 年
期固定房貸平均利率攀升至 7.33%(高於上月的 6.86%及去年同期的 6.25%)，使購
屋負擔加重，全美住房可負擔指數亦由去年同期的 107.3 降至 100.7。觀察區域表
現，10 月西部、南部及中西部銷量分別月減 9.7%、6.5%及 3.2%，僅東北部維持
持平，且四大地區銷量均較去年同期衰退。儘管買氣受抑，全美成屋售價中位數
仍年增 0.4%至 41.67 萬美元，寫下連續 40 個月年增紀錄；供給端則持續擴增，
待售庫存升至 159 萬戶，月增 1.3%、年增 4.6%，相當於 5.1 個月供給量(高於上
月的 4.7 個月與去年同期的 4.4 個月)。全美房地產經紀人協會(NAR)首席經濟學
家 Lawrence Yun 指出，在高利率衝擊下，10 月成屋市場呈現「量縮、價微揚、
庫存增」的格局，但全國 8,800 萬名屋主整體財務狀況依然穩健，不良資產交易
僅占 2%，考量目前就業市場仍持續成長，未來一旦房貸利率回落，市場可望釋

5

出可觀的積壓買氣。 
2026 年 10 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，低於上月之
0.6%，惟高於市場預期的 0.3%；扣除食品與能源價格之核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，與
前月持平，並高於市場預期的 0.2%。在細項方面，住房指數月增 0.3%，貢獻整
體 CPI 月增幅逾三成，仍為推升通膨的重要因素；食品指數月增 0.5%，其中家庭
食品及外食價格分別上漲 0.4%及 0.5%；能源指數月增 0.7%，其中汽油價格及電
價分別上漲 1.0%及 0.9%，惟汽油漲幅較上月明顯放緩。核心項目方面，休閒娛
樂、醫療照護、汽車保險、家庭用品及教育等價格持續上揚，二手車及卡車價格
則下跌 1.2%，通訊價格亦小幅下降。另受 2025 年 10 月美國政府停擺影響，當月
未進行 CPI 調查並缺乏比較基期資料，美國勞工局未發布 2026 年 10 月整體 CPI
及多數主要指數之年增率。整體而言，10 月美國物價仍受住房、食品及能源價格
上漲帶動，核心 CPI 月增率則連續第三個月維持 0.3%，顯示服務類通膨壓力仍具
韌性，後續通膨走勢仍須持續觀察能源價格及住房成本變化。 
2026 年 10 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 0.3%，明顯低於上
月修正後之 1.4%，惟年增率由 6.3%升至 6.5%，創 2022 年 11 月以來最大漲幅，
顯示生產端價格壓力仍高。其中，最終需求商品價格月增 0.5%，低於上月之 2.5%，
逾六成漲幅來自不含食品及能源之商品價格上升，其中基本有機化學品價格上漲
3.9%；食品價格亦月增 1.3%，惟能源價格微降 0.1%，其中柴油價格下跌 7.9%。
另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.2%，低於上月之 1.0%，主要受運輸及倉儲
服務價格上漲 2.0%帶動，其中貨運卡車運輸價格上漲 3.9%，貢獻服務價格漲幅
逾八成，惟貿易服務價格下降 0.2%，抵銷部分漲幅。剔除食品、能源及貿易服務
後之核心最終需求 PPI 月增 0.5%，與上月持平，年增率則由 5.0%小幅降至 4.9%。
整體而言，10 月美國 PPI 月增幅明顯放緩，主要反映能源價格回落及服務價格漲
勢降溫，惟食品、部分非能源商品及貨運價格仍持續上漲，加上整體 PPI 年增率
進一步攀升，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍高，後續須關注企業成本轉嫁情形、能源及
運輸價格變化，以及對消費者物價與聯準會利率政策之影響。 
2026 年亞太經濟合作組織(APEC)領袖峰會將於 11 月 18 日至 19 日在中國深
圳舉行。在峰會召開前及「川習三會」前夕，中美雙方相繼延長部分關稅豁免與
暫停措施，釋出緩和貿易緊張關係的訊號，並為後續經貿協商創造較有利條件。
中國宣布自 11 月 10 日起續暫停實施 24%的對美加徵關稅兩個月，並保留 10%稅

率作為後續談判籌碼，同時延長對美船舶特別港務費、出口管制及部分反制措施
的暫停期限；美國則將 178 項中國商品的 301 關稅豁免延長至 2027 年 1 月 9 日，
涵蓋工業產品、太陽能設備及電子零組件等項目，並同步延後對中國船舶港口費
及岸橋設備加徵關稅措施。此番互釋善意反映雙方均有意維持經貿關係穩定，降
低企業經營及供應鏈不確定性，並為 APEC 期間的高層對話創造有利條件；惟中
方仍保留部分關稅措施，顯示未完全放棄反制工具，後續發展仍將視雙方談判進
展而定。 
輝達(NVIDIA)創辦人黃仁勳近日在被爆出與疑涉中國情報背景的女子黃樂
(Alice Huang)有婚外情並於 11 月 8 日坦承對妻子不忠致歉後，因外部與董事會調
查持續延燒，且《紐約時報》與《路透社》接連揭露其與直屬下屬輝達醫療保健
副總裁金伯莉·鮑爾(Kimberly Powell)及另一名內部女員工也存在婚外情，最終於
11 月 12 日正式宣布辭去總裁、執行長與董事會職位，結束其掌舵輝達 33 年的傳
奇生涯，並由財務長克雷斯(Colette Kress)暫代執行長兵符，同時任命哈維·瓊斯
(Harvey C. Jones)為首任董事會主席。同時，美國總統川普於 11 月 9 日證實聯邦
調查局(FBI)已介入調查該起事件及潛在的反情報國安疑慮，並表示雖與黃仁勳通
話且相信其不知女方背景的說法，仍會交由情報部門進一步核實。黃仁勳閃辭的
消息隨即震撼全球科技與金融市場，導致輝達股價在 11 月 12 日開盤後盤中一度
重挫逾 14%、單日市值一度蒸發約 6,000 億美元，不僅重創高「含輝量」台股 ETF
表現，更引發外界對全球 AI 產業版圖及美中科技供應鏈穩定性的高度憂慮。儘
管黃仁勳矽谷盟友與部分員工近期發起連署與遊說，試圖推動他重返輝達，但美
國財政部長貝森特(Scott Bessent)已公開呼籲輝達領導層應進行「大幅改組」，使
後續接班與公司治理動向備受矚目。 
二、歐元區—勞動市場開始出現疲態 
 歐元區第三季 GDP 季增率二次估計值維持 0.4%不變，顯示在地緣政治不確
定性升高、能源價格再度上揚的環境下，整體景氣仍具支撐力；今年前三季經濟
成長率(年化率)則大約為 1.1%，大致持平去年水準。從需求面來看，政府支出與
民間消費為主要成長動能，其中民間消費受惠於較高的儲蓄水準及穩定的就業市
場，減輕能源價格上漲的衝擊，並且電動車、油電混合車需求持續增加，抵銷利
率升高及地緣政治波動對投資帶來的負面影響。

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

展望未來，從歐元區採購經理人指標(PMI)顯示第四季經濟仍可望延續溫和擴
張，在能源衝擊逐漸消退後，2027 年成長動能有望進一步增強。不過，第三季就
業呈現零成長，主要是德、法等核心國家就業疲弱，抵銷南歐國家就業增加的影
響，勞動市場可能逐漸走疲為未來復甦的隱憂。若就業狀況不如預期，恐進一步
抑制薪資表現，並削弱消費信心，進而影響經濟復甦。 
三、日本—經濟成長停滯，內外需動能轉弱 
 2026 年第三季經濟成長季增率持平，結束連續三季的經濟擴張，主要受通膨、
部分地區自然災害及全球需求疲弱影響，導致私人消費季減 0.3%，出口亦下滑
0.2%，加上進口反彈 2.5%，使淨貿易拖累 GDP 達 0.6 個百分點；惟企業投資季
增 1.6%，為近三季以來首次成長，對景氣形成一定支撐。整體而言，日本內外需
動能均有所轉弱，後續民間消費回溫及企業投資能否持續，將是觀察經濟能否恢
復成長的重要指標。 
四、中國—國際能源價格上漲抑制貿易順差擴張 
10 月出口年增率較 9 月續增 1.6 個百分點至 37.9%，出口總值為 4,123 億美
元；累計前十個月出口年增率上升為 22.7%，累積出口總值為 37,827 億美元。前
十個月出口主要成長項目(累積年增率)包含 IC(111.1%)、自動數據處理設備
(60.6%)、船舶(27.7%)、汽車(52.2%)，其中 IC 及自動數據處理設備出口加速成長，
為 10 月出口年增率加速上升的主要因素。 
10 月進口年增率較 9 月增 10.6 個百分點至 41.6%，進口總值為 3,071 億美
元；累計前十個月進口值成長 29%至 27,401 億美元。該月原油、成品油進口金額
大幅增加，為帶動進口加速成長的主要因素；IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器
等)進口分別成長 65.1%、136.7%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部分伺服器
關鍵組件有賴於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前十個月貿易順差 10,426 億美
元，僅較去年同期增加 8.7%，增幅明顯低於出口，顯示出口擴張同時伴隨關鍵原
物料進口大幅增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價值產品依賴
國際供應鏈支持。此外，因貿易順差增幅有限，10 月外匯存底僅增 49 億美元至
約 3.41 兆美元。 
10 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 9 月的 5%升至 5.7%，主因原油價格居高不下，
燃料購進成本持續上漲，提高生產成本所致。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格

8

年增率續揚 10.5 個百分點至 35%，並帶動燃料加工業出廠價格年增率上揚至
29.9%，此外，對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學品、化纖出廠價格年增率加速升
至 16.4%、16%，顯示原油價格上漲的影響開始擴散至中下游產業。此外，由於
近期鋼鐵價格走弱，黑色金屬礦產開採出廠價格年減 6.4%，為主要拖累因素。 
10 月消費者通膨(CPI)、核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別回落 0.2 個百分點為
0.7%、0.8%。儘管燃油通膨續增 5.3 個百分點至 20.2%，但食品、房租、汽車、
旅遊價格漲幅皆有放緩，其他商品及服務價格則大致平穩，使消費者通膨較前月
下滑。整體而言，雖然企業生產成本持續上升，但在民間消費乏力之下，企業仍
大多自行吸收成本，加上政府控制零售燃油及民生水電價格漲幅，故目前原油價
格高漲僅少數反映於消費端。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，若油價維持高檔
或進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度通膨超標；印尼、菲律賓第三季 GDP 分別達 5.5%及
2.1% 
(1)印度：10 月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增率進一步上揚至 6.09%，遠高於 9 月
的 5.54%，其中食品通膨加速上升至 8.5%，反映聖嬰現象導致季風降雨量低於預
期，影響農作物收成，同時核心通膨率（剔除汽油、柴油和貴金屬）從 9 月的 3.2%
上升至 3.8%，顯示潛在通膨持續走強並有所擴大。由於通膨率高於預期且突破央
行 6%的目標區間上限，預期印度央行 12 月的政策會議將有進一步升息的壓力。 
(2)印尼：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 5.47%，私人消費成長 5.04%，主
要因政府採取措施，協助家庭抵禦通膨壓力和更高的借貸成本，惟受能源補貼負
擔加重導致財政空間收窄，政府支出大幅放緩至 8.95%，遠低於上季的 15.97%；
固定資產投資成長略放緩至 6.06%；另受石油和能源成本上漲影響，進口成長率
（7.31%）高於出口成長率（4.59%），致淨出口產生負貢獻，惟較上季對經濟的
拖累有所減緩。按產業別觀之，製造業(5.87%)、住宿業（11.22%）、運輸/倉儲業
（8.03%）和通訊業（7.13%）均有所成長，採礦業產出由負轉正 0.9%，抵銷農業
產出受聖嬰現象影響而大幅下降至 0.5%的影響。整體而言，第三季經濟成長優於
預期，惟在財政和貨幣政策收緊的背景下，國內需求可能進一步放緩。 
(3)菲律賓：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 2.1%，為 2009 年第四季以來（不
計新冠疫情影響）最疲軟的經濟成長，低於政府先前已下調的全年 3.5%至 4.5%

的目標，聖嬰現象令農業產量低迷和能源成本高漲持續抑制經濟活動。家庭消費
成長放緩至 2.4%，政府支出成長降至 6.8%，固定投資雖小幅復甦仍萎縮 4.4%，
所幸出口成長 8.6%、進口成長放緩至 7.7%，致淨出口呈正向貢獻。按產業別觀
之，工業成長加快至 5.4%，服務業小幅放緩至 9%，惟農業產出受氣候影響而衰
退 0.4%。整體而言，第三季經濟活動主要受氣候干擾，農業、服務產出下滑並影
響消費支出，固定投資在公共建設投資帶動下有好轉跡象，然而央行升息提高借
貸成本，對私人投資產構成壓力，隨極端天氣事件的影響逐漸消退、家庭在年底
假期前消費意願提升、以及政府加快基礎設施建設，將有助於第四季經濟加快復
甦。 
六、台灣—AI 商機持續發酵，出口動能強勁 
10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，年增 58.9%，創歷史新高，主要由 AI 及高效能運
算需求帶動，其中資通與視聽產品、電子零組件合計約占總出口 83%，顯示 AI 伺
服器、半導體及相關零組件已成為出口成長核心。同時，對美出口達 329.3 億美
元、年增 55.9%，東協亦大幅成長 83.8%，反映 AI 需求及全球供應鏈重組正帶動
出口市場結構改變。惟進口年增 75.1%，增幅高於出口，後續宜觀察進口增加是
否有效轉化為產能及出口，以及 AI 需求、高基期與全球科技業資本支出變化對
出口動能的影響。

1 群益投顧 
11/23/2026 台積電(2330 TT)罕見在非法說會期間打破沉默，發出聲明，重申全球 AI
需 求 與 日 俱 增 ， 先 進 製 程 產 能 供 不 應 求 的 情 況 依 然 存 在 。 雖 然 市 場 擔 心 AI 數據中心
投 資 可 能 延 後 ， 但 在 能 源 和 資 源 限 制 下 ， 反 而 有 利 先 進 製 程 需 求 提 升 ， 因 此 對 台 積 電
投資建議 Buy。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw

近期市場對 AI 產 業 前 景 出 現 雜 音 ， 11/23/2026 台 積 電 罕 見 在 非 法 說 會 期 間
打破沉默，發 出 聲 明 ， 重 申 全球 AI 需 求 與 日 俱 增 ， 先 進 製 程 產 能 供 不 應 求
的 情 況 依 然 存 在 。  
台 積 電 指 出 ， AI 應 用 正 從 聊 天 機 器 人 (Chatbot)轉向 AI 助理(AI Assistant)，
代理式 AI(Agentic AI)日 益 普 及 ， 將 進 一 步 推 升 整 體 算 力 與 晶 片 需 求 ； 而 無
論 客 戶 最 終 採 用 何 種 架 構 方 案，都 需 要 最 先 進 的 半 導 體 製 程 技 術 作 為 後 盾。 
儘 管 消 費 性 ( 如 PC、 智 慧 型 手 機 ) 與 價 格 敏 感 終 端 市 場 面 臨 零 組 件 價 格 上 漲
與 總 體 經 濟 不 確 定 性 之 挑 戰 ， 台 積 電 對 2027 年 對此抱 持 審 慎 樂觀態度，但
受惠於領先先進製程之技術差異化與廣泛客戶基礎，及代理人 AI(Agentic 
AI) 推動 AI 助 理 普 及 、 開 源 模 型 降 低 使 用 門 檻 ， 進 一 步 刺 激 CPU 與 網 路 晶
片需求， 預 估 台 積 電 2027 年 稅 後 EPS 147.10 元。11/11/2026 台 積 電 資 深 副
總 經 理 暨 副 共 同 營 運 長 張 曉 強 出 席 Nikkei Forum Global Management 
Dialogue 2026 ， 會 中 表 示 台 積 電 的 客 戶 ， 甚 至 客 戶 的 客 戶 ， 認 為 算 力 為 AI
時 代 最 重 要 的 資 源 ， 客 戶 持 續 轉 向 更 先 進 的 製 程 。 雖 然 市 場 擔 心 AI 數 據 中
心 投 資 可 能 延 後，但 在 能 源 和 資 源 限 制 下，反 而 有 利 先 進 製 程 需 求 提 升，因
此 對 台 積 電 投 資 建 議 Buy， 目 標 價 2,945 元。

產業速報

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

For Pegatron, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$514bn (+32% QoQ, +61% YoY), as 
we expect to see more contribution from AI server going into 4Q26. We expect GM to 
slightly decrease to 4.2% (-0.3ppt QoQ and +0.1%ppt YoY). We continue to see 4Q26 
revenue with QoQ growth on a continuing ramp-up in AI server shipments as well as the 
continued peak season for its communication business. 
For Compal, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$282bn (+16% QoQ, +48% YoY) with 
increasing contribution from AI server  as the company continue to expand from 
motherboard (L6) to system level (L10/L11). We expect GM to slightly increase to 4.6% 
(+0.2ppt QoQ and -1.2ppt YoY) as the YoY contraction is due to shift in product mix (we 
expect PC business to account for 60% in 4Q26 vs. 71% in 4Q25).  
For Asus, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$283bn (+2% QoQ, +40% YoY), with solid 
growth from its server business, offsetting the weakness from its PC business, although 
we continue to expect a more premium PC product mix. We expect GM to decrease to 
14.8% (-1.6ppt QoQ and -1.1ppt YoY) due to the change in product mix.

### Page 3

Earnings revisions 
Quanta 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 3% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
up our 2026E revenue estimates by 2%). We revise down 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 7%/1% to reflect a product mix with higher contribution from the server business (we 
revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 1/2%). We now expect the GM to contract 
going forward due to the higher server business contribution, thus revise down GPM by 
0.3/0.2/0.1ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Pegatron 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 20% to factor in 3Q26 results (we 
maintain our 2026E revenue estimates). We revise down 2027 earnings estimates by 5% 
to reflect a higher % for minority interest while revise up 2028 earnings estimates by 3% 
with improved GM (we revise down our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 9/6% to reflect 
a more conservative assumption for its communication business). We now expect the 
GM to remain stable going forward, thus revise up GPM by 0.4/0.7/0.5ppt in 
2026/27/28E.

### Page 4

Compal 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 10% to factor in 3Q26 results (our 
2026E revenue estimates change immaterially). We revise down 2027/28E earnings 
estimates by 4/7% to factor in the impact from minority interest (we revise up our 
2027/28E revenue estimates by 9/9% to reflect a more constructive assumption on its 
server business). We now expect the GM to remain stable going forward, thus only revise 
down 0.1ppt in 2026E.

Asus 
We revise up our 2026 earnings estimates by 11% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise up 
our 2026E revenue estimates by 2%). We revise down 2027/28E earnings estimates by 
11/15% to factor in the impact from minority interest (we revise up our 2027/28E 
revenue estimates by 3/4% to reflect a higher contribution from its server business). We 
now expect the GM to decline going forward with higher server contribution, thus revise 
down 0.5/0.8ppt in 2027/28E.

Valuation 
Quanta 
Maintain Neutral on Quanta, with a new TP of NT$280 (from NT$300), based on the 
same 10.7x 2027E EPS (which is the company’s 2018-23 average P/E), and factoring in 
our latest earnings revisions.

Pegatron 
Maintain Sell on Pegatron, with a new TP of NT$71 (from NT$73.3), based on the same 
9.7x 2027E EPS (which is the company’s 2018-23 average P/E), and factoring in our 
latest earnings revisions.

Compal 
Maintain Neutral on Compal, with a new TP of NT$45 (from NT$46.1), based on the 
same 14.7x 2027E EPS (which is +0.5 STDV above the company’s 2018-23 average P/E), 
and factoring in our latest earnings revisions.

Asus

and factoring in our latest earnings revisions.

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Quanta 
Quanta is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, tablets, 
consumer electronics, servers, etc. We are Neutral rated on Quanta. We expect the 
company’s earnings to grow in 2026-28E, driven by 1) growing AI server demand from 
Quanta’s CSP customers, and 2) general server demand recovery. However, we see P/E 
valuation as fair at the current level, given the positives appear to have been mostly 
factored in the share price and the current upside to our target price is relatively lower 
compared to its peers under coverage. We would consider becoming more positive if the 
AI server demand from its CSP clients turn out to be above our expectations.

### Page 7

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$280 is based on a 
10.7x 2027E P/E, which is the company’s 2018-23 average P/E.  
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers. 
Pegatron is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of 
smartphones, PCs, consumer electronics, and servers, etc. While we like Pegatron’s 
product line diversification into servers, AI servers, and automotive electronics, we are 
Sell rated as we see revenue contribution from AI server remaining small in near-term, 
and our 2027E target P/E is near the company’s current trading P/E. We expect 
smartphone business will remain the major revenue contributor from 2026E to 2028E, 
and we see the AI server related revenue contribution remains 4% in 2026E (vs. peers 
14%~87%). Catalysts for further upside will be its faster-than-expected progress in AI 
servers. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Pegatron 
We are Sell rated on Pegatron with a 12-month target price of NT$71. Our TP is based 
on a 9.7x 2027E P/E, which is the company’s 2018-23 average P/E. 
Key upside risks: 1) stronger-than-expected smartphone market recovery, 2) 
faster-than-expected AI server ramp-up, and 3) stronger-than-expected demand on 
consumer electronics.

Investment thesis- Compal 
Compal is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, tablets, 
consumer electronics, servers, etc. While we like Compal’s solid position in the PC ODM 
market and its expansion into AI servers, we are Neutral rated as we see the contribution 
from AI servers as limited in the near-term given its limited exposure to cloud server 
customers, who are the early adopters of AI servers. Meanwhile, we believe valuation 
already reflects near-term positives. We model Compal earnings growth in 2026-26E to 
be mainly driven by commercial PC demand recovery and server business ramping up. 
We see P/E valuation as fair at current levels compared to peers given its mild earnings 
growth in 2026-28E.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Compal 
We are Neutral-rated on Compal with a 12-month target price of NT$45. Our target 
price is based on 14.7x 2027E P/E, which is +0.5 STDV above the company’s 2018-23 
average P/E. 
Key upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger/weaker-than-expected demand for tablets.

### Page 8

Investment thesis- Asus 
Asus is a multinational computer hardware and electronics company based in Taiwan 
and is the top motherboard and gaming brand globally and also a top-three consumer 
notebook vendor, per the company. We are Neutral rated on Asus considering (1) 
challenging PC market under end of Win10 replacement cycle, rising memory costs, and 
consumption pull in in 1H25 under China government subsidies and tariff concerns, (2) 
ASUS brand AI servers remain small in shipment. The stock currently trades at 14x 2027E 
PE, close to our target PE multiple (14.8x), which we think is fair.  
We would turn more positive on ASUS if we see (1) its PC revenue growth stronger than 
expected which could be driven by faster than expected product mix upgrade toward AI 
PC or gaming PC, or (2) faster than expected AI servers shipment ramp up, which could 
be driven by penetration in leading enterprises or CSPs (cloud services providers).

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Asus 
We are Neutral-rated on ASUS with a 12-month target price of NT$1,000. Our target 
price is based on 14.8x 2027E P/E, which is +0.5 STDV above the company’s 2018-23 
average P/E 
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger / weaker-than-expected PC market 
growth; 2) faster / slower-than-expected AI and gaming PC growth; and 3) faster / 
slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

• Mix and UTR lift margins - 3Q26 revenue grew about 50% YoY, with every 
product line, region and application higher in absolute terms. GPM returned 
above 40% to 41.0%, up 2.5ppts QoQ, while gross profit and net income 
rose far higher than its top line. Mgmt. credited AI high-end products, 
higher UTR and internal efficiency gains alongside pricing. UTR exceeded 
90% across standard and specialty lines. MLCC margins now rank above 
inductors and sit close to the corp. average, a clear sign of mix upgrade. For 
4Q26, mgmt. guided slight growth in revenue, GPM and OPM, with the BB 
ratio near 2 and Oct-26 revenue still up 3% MoM in a holiday-heavy month.
• 2027 expansion targets the tightest lines - Yageo has approved a 10-15% 
group capacity expansion for 2027, with equipment already ordered. The 
additions re concentrated in MLCC and tantalum, currently the two tightest 
product lines, and further projects remain under review. Mgmt. expects the 
supply gap to persist in 2027, with market growth supported by data center 
projects already approved and under construction, although visibility into 
2028 could be slightly lower. AI now accounts for 20% of total revenue, 
with tantalum, resistor, MLCC having the highest exposure. Yageo has kept 
serving standard orders throughout the cycle, since customers who switch 
suppliers rarely return once requalification is complete.
• Pricing discipline and flexible LTAs - The Jul-26 price adjustment is 
flowing through to contract customers over 1-2 quarters, with a larger 
contribution in 4Q26 and a full run-rate from 1Q27. Mgmt. will not rule out 
further adjustments if the market stays tight. LTAs carry a pricing 
framework that floats with the market, with no ceiling and no customer 
prepayment, and they are capped at 40% of capacity to preserve flexibility. 
Channel inventory remains healthy. Distributors in the US and EU are still 
restocking industrial and aerospace inventory from below safety levels,

Yageo
Valuation
We set our target price at NT$1,000, based on 23x average 2027E-28E earnings. We value Yageo based on PE as we believe the
company’s earnings performance and industry dynamics would be the key focuses of the market. Our target multiple of 23x is
at the higher end of the trading range since 2022 but in line with global peers average. During this period the company has
gone through the cycles, successfully integrated the acquired business to enhance its product offerings, and started to deliver

stable margins and earnings.
Risks
Key downside risks to our target price and rating include: 1) slower-than-expected demand recovery; 2) intensified peer and
pricing competition; 3) execution risks associated with M&A integration and synergies; and 4) FX and interest rate fluctuations.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

1 
4540 全球傳動(TBI MOTION)

報告日期：2026/11/19 研 究 員：洪 國 峰 (Titan Horng) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 財報受惠產品組合優化，呆帳回沖，業外匯兌利益挹注，
單季 EPS 0.79 元，優於先前預期 0.44 元。 
 蘇州新廠將於 1Q~2Q27 正式投產，將有助舒緩台灣樹林廠產
能吃緊情況。 
 桃園大溪廠己於 4Q26 正式處分，預計 1Q27 交割完成，處分
利益 1.1 億元，貢獻全球傳動 EPS 0.95 元。 
一. 公告 3Q26 稅後 EPS 0.79 元 
在客戶訂單回溫，全球傳動 3Q26 營收金額 9.46 億元，
YoY+40.27%，QoQ+13.96%，營業利益 0.81 億元，較 3Q25 由
虧轉盈，QoQ+63.74%，稅後盈餘 0.92 億 元，較 3Q25 由虧轉盈，
QoQ+80.7%，稅後 EPS 0.79 元。1Q~3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.04 元，
優於去年同期-1.08 元。 
二. 預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 0.56 元 
全球 10/2026 營收金額 2.88 億元，YoY+11.5%，MoM-11.14%。
全球傳動 4Q26 將致力優化產品組合為目標，預估全球傳動 4Q26
營收金額 9.36 億元，YoY+21.5%，QoQ-1.05%，預估營業利益
0.59 億元，較 4Q25 由虧轉盈，QoQ-27.6%，預估稅後盈餘 0.64
億元，YoY+38.2%，QoQ-30.1%，預估稅後 EPS 0.56 元。 
三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.60 元 
全球傳動 2026 年傳動元件產品如行星滾柱螺桿與微型螺桿持續
切入機器人，半導體與自動化設備領域，產能利用率回溫，帶動
由虧轉盈。預估全球傳動 2026 年營收金額 35 億元，YoY+36.8%，
預估稅後盈餘 1.84 億元，由虧轉盈，預估稅後 EPS 1.60 元。 
四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.30 元 
全球傳動 2027 年在中國大陸持續大力發展機器人領域，將有助全
球行星滾柱螺桿與微型螺桿產品切入，另外大溪廠處分利益 1Q27
認列，有助全球傳動 2027 年營運表現。預估全球傳動 2027 年營
收金額 40 億元，YoY+14.3% ，預估稅後盈餘 3.80 億元，
YoY+105.97%，預估稅後 EPS 3.30 元。 
五. 投資建議買進，目標價 66 元 
觀察機器人執行機構件包括旋轉執行器，線性執行器，末端執行
器，全球傳動己小量成功打入線性執行器與靈巧手廠商供應鏈，
將有助評價提升，故投資建議買進，目標價 66 元。(28X 2027 年
扣除大溪廠土地後稅後 EPS=20X 2027 年稅後 EPS )

2

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
全球傳動科技(TBI MOTION)成立於 2010 年，資 本 額 11.52 億元，
並於 2018 年掛牌上市，全球傳動為台灣傳動元件專業製造廠，TBI 
MOTION 掌握關鍵核心技術，專注創新研發與設計，擁有多項產
品設計專利並且通過 ISO9001 認證，經銷營業據點分佈全球，廣
獲各國客戶的肯定。TBI MOTION 產品線齊全、MIT 台灣製造生
產，主要產品：滾珠螺桿、線性滑軌、滾珠花鍵、旋轉式滾珠螺
桿/花鍵、單軸機器人、直線軸承、聯軸器、螺桿支撐座..等。 產
品的應用範圍非常廣泛，實際應用於自動化產業、半導設備、產
業機械、工具機產業、綠能產業與機器人相關產業。全球傳動營
收地區方面，中國大陸 74~82%、台灣地區 7~10%、亞洲地區
6~10%、歐洲地區 3~5%、美洲地區 1%。

(單軸機器人)。

圖二、全球傳動產品

資料來源：公司&宏遠投顧彙整

預估 2027 年滾珠螺桿營收金額 21.00 億元，YoY+12.9%

滾珠螺桿主要功用在於藉由鋼珠於螺桿與螺帽之間進行運動，由
於滾珠螺桿在頻繁返復運轉時，鋼珠間會產生摩擦熱度導致出現
氧化或脫碳現象而縮短使用壽命，全球傳動滾珠螺桿以轉造級螺
桿為主，所提供標準規格為 6mm~80mm，亦可提供研磨級螺桿規
格為 4mm~160mm，螺帽部分則以循環方式區分內循環、外循環、
端蓋式循環。滾珠螺桿 2025 年營收金額 14.73 億元，
YoY+0.00%，在中國大陸製造業景氣低迷，導致客戶訂單量維持
在低檔水準。

4

滾珠螺桿 1Q~3Q26 營收金額 13.59 億元，YoY+33.27%。預 期 2026
年，全球傳動己切入人形機器人的關鍵零組件，包括線型執行器
與靈巧手。觀察一台人型機器人需要用到 14 個線型執行器，一個
線型執行器需要一台行星滾柱螺桿；靈巧手方面，一台人型機器
人需要兩隻靈巧手，每隻靈巧手需要 15 支微型滾珠螺桿。上述都
己分別打入台灣與中國大陸機器人相關供應鏈，預期 2026 年開始
小量出貨。另外半導體則切入後段封裝設備廠，預期機器人及半
導體可望為營運成長動能。宏遠預估 2026 年滾珠螺桿相關產品營
收金額 18.60 億元，YoY+2633%。

預期 2027 年，在滾珠螺桿在自動化設備與機器人市場規模持續擴
大帶動下，另外微小型研磨螺桿己打入 CPO 設備模組供應鏈，將
有助滾珠螺桿出貨持續成長，宏遠預估 2027 年滾珠螺桿相關產品
營收金額 21.00 億元，YoY+12.9%。

預估 2027 年線性滑軌營收金額 16.35 億元，YoY+14.3%

線性滑軌屬滾動導引裝置，主要藉由鋼珠於滑塊與滑軌之間作無
限滾動循環，以維持負載平台順沿滑軌進行高精度線性運動，其
中技術層次要求須將線性滑軌摩擦係數降至傳統滑動導引的
1/50，以及滑塊與滑軌間末制單元設計須能同時承受各方向負
荷。全球傳動標準型線性滑軌尺吋涵蓋 15mm~65mm，另可提供
7mm~15mm 之微小型滑軌。線性滑軌 2025 年營收金額 9.41 億
元，YoY+35.2%。

預估 2027 年滾珠花鍵與其他營收金額 2.65 億元，YoY+26.2%

滾珠花鍵特色相較於滾珠花鍵可大負荷容量、旋轉方向零間隙、
高度靈敏性、高剛性與裝配簡單等。其他產品主要以單軸機器人
為主，單軸機器人採用線軌與螺桿的優點，將螺帽與滑塊設計成
一體式機構，並搭配高剛性的 U 型軌最佳化斷面，可達到最佳的
空間節省及大幅度減少組裝的時間，確保其高剛性、高精度要求。
滾珠花鍵與其他產品 2025 年營收金額 1.46 億元，YoY+16.1%。

在自動化智慧工廠趨勢明確，全球將持續開發新產品，加上產能
擴充帶動，將有助滾珠花鍵與單軸機器人恢復成長動能，預估滾
珠花鍵與其他 2026 年營收金額 2.1 億元，YoY+44.03%。

矽力*-KY (6415 TT) 
明年營收成長性可望達 30%

永豐觀點 
資料中心與車載為成長主軸，兩者總計明年營收比重可望達 45%。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年稅後 EPS 10.92 元(原預估 11.95 元)，2027
年稅後 EPS 15.32 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營運拓展，研究部
維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析 
矽力*-KY 企業永續評鑑整體為 B 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面
向平均得分分別為 B+等與 B 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2023 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。

### Page 2

2

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 3Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為五大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)34%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)37%、資訊產品(如筆電)7%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)7%、車載 15%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營業費用較高：矽力杰公布 3Q26 營收 72.59 億元(+21%QoQ，+52%YoY)，
毛利率 47.58%，稅後獲利 9.09 億元(-42%QoQ，+34%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.31 元，
財報不如預期，主要是營業費用規模偏高。矽力杰 3Q26 財報細項指標如下：(1) 
營收 72.59 億元(+21%QoQ，+52%YoY)，此係來自資料中心訂單加持、車載上
升(新款晶片推出與市占率提升)、消費電子季增 14%(即便市場需求疲弱，但因公
司新款晶片推出，故反而仍能向上)、資通訊產品下滑(因記憶體報價上漲，推高終
端售價而影響消費力道)。(2) 毛利率由 2Q26 的 47.03%微增至 47.42%，符合公
司原預期。(3) 營業費用 24.78 億元，季增達 19%，主要是銷售費用、資料中心應
用研發費用、3Q26 應屆畢業生招募人力增加。(4) 業外損失約 1100 萬元，相較
2Q26 業外收益 9.58 億元出現明顯差異，主要是前期有加特蘭微電子的再評價增
值利益產生。(5) 存貨 70.36 億元，季增 23%，存貨天數 150 天，達到歷史上緣位
置，公司表示係因封測供給吃緊，導致封測交期拉長(部分達到六個月)，因此 wafer bank
囤積在封測廠，導致庫存水位增加。

4Q26 營收持平到小增，略有不如預期：矽力杰預期(1) 4Q26 營收持平到小增(研
究部原預估 QoQ+5%~6%)，其中營運動能仍以資料中心為成長主軸，不過消費
電子市況相當疲弱(公司推測可能與客戶因應原物料與供應鏈不確定性，而提前出
貨，導致 4Q26 拉貨動能驟減)，包括手機與 TV 均顯弱勢。(2) 毛利率隨產品結構
變化、Gen4 製程平台滲透率提升至 20%，使得毛利率應能優於 3Q26 水準。
(3)4Q26~1H27 營業費用建議以 3Q26 的水準計算。研究部預估矽力杰 4Q26 營
收 74.29 億元(+2%QoQ，+38%YoY)，毛利率 47.84%，稅後獲利 10.5 億元
(+16%QoQ，+30%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.67 元。 
2027 年營收成長性落於 YoY+20%~+30%上緣，反映成長力道延續：矽力杰維持
預期資料中心、車載與為主要成長推手(兩者 2027 年營收總計 YoY+>50%)，並給
予更明確的營收比重目標值：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營
收比重 17%，2027 年底預計可達 25%。公司資料中心三大項目比重依序為光通
訊>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重
雖較低，不過主板業務(包括控制晶片與 SPS)的新款晶片目前客戶均已處驗證中，
預計 2027 年將放量(公司表是北美客戶的 SPS 需求快於中國)。公司表示儘管近期
資料中心有諸多因素干擾，不過客戶針對明年訂單需求仍相當穩固。(2) 車載營收
比重可望由現階段 15%，攀升至 2027 年的 20%(原預估 15%~20%)，主要動能
來自於 content value 提升(由 200~200USD 往 270~300USD 邁進，係來自 MCU
與高電壓/大電流晶片出貨)、中國電動車的海外銷售攀升。(3) 公司期待隨 Gen4
製程平台 2027 年滲透率達 50%、Gen5 製程平台量產與產品結構持續往資料中
心、車載布局，對 2027 年毛利率>50%有信心。不過研究部認為矽力杰過往毛利
率容易受到存貨損失調整(因庫齡政策)影響，又製造成本上漲壓力延續，故雖預估
2027 年毛利率趨勢向上，但上升的幅度以較保守的態度推估。

維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 255.51 億元(+36%YoY)，毛利率
47.6%，稅後獲利 42.98 億元(+72%YoY)，稅後 EPS 10.92 元(原預估 11.95 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 338.3 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.98%，稅後獲利 60.32 億
元(+40%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.32 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營
運拓展，研究部維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

## Document 191: 6515穎崴 亞東

Source PDF: all report pdf/6515穎崴 亞東.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

◼ 投資建議: 
美系 AI GPU 與客製化 ASIC 持續開案，帶動高階測試座需求。模型預估，穎崴的 NVIDIA 
系統級測試座（SLT）營收將由 2026 年的 33.26 億元增至 2027 年的 83.45 億元，貢獻公
司逾半營收增量。公司目標於 2027 上半年將自製彈簧針月產能提升至 1,400 萬針，有助降
低外購成本，支撐全年毛利率由 40.92% 升至 42.97%。穎崴的矽光子與 CPO 測試方案已涵蓋
晶圓、封裝及模組，若客戶專案進入量產，可望帶來額外營收。 
根據模型，穎崴(6515)目標價 5,711 元。依模型預估的 2027 年 EPS 163.18 元 × 基準本益比 
35 倍估算，較 2026/11/18 收盤價 3,560 元具 60.4% 潛在上漲空間。建議買進 
◼ 未來觀察： 
1. 探針自製率&產能狀況 
2. HPC/AI 業務成長性 
◼ 公司是否涉及經營權爭奪之情事：無。 
◼ 產業概況： 
1. 半導體測試介面市場:系統測試需求高速成長。 
AI GPU、伺服器 CPU 與雲端業者自研 ASIC 擴張，帶動高階晶片封裝後最終測試（FT）與
系統級測試（SLT）需求成長。FT 把關量產電性品質；SLT 模擬實際系統與軟體運作，補足 
FT 對系統整合、長時間與極端負載之測試不足，隨晶片複雜度提升，滲透率持續走高。 
市場預估全球高階 HPC FT/SLT Socket TAM 將自 2026E 9 億美元躍升至 2028E 37 億美元，
CAGR 約 104%（SLT 達約 110%）。動能來自時間與耗材乘數效應：2025–2028E AI 晶片 
FT/SLT 測試時間 CAGR 達 15–20%（如 Rubin SLT 增至 120–150+ 分鐘），壓低單槽產出
並迫使客戶擴增平行測試槽位。隨大封裝、萬級以上高 Pin 數、高頻高速及破千瓦功耗成為
主流，高階測試座須整合訊號完整性、大電流與溫控散熱，帶動 FT Socket 單顆價值量提升 2 
至 3 倍；長時高功耗壓接亦加速探針磨損與更換，創造 4 至 5 倍耗材數量乘數效應。

### Page 2

◼ 公司簡介： 
1. 穎崴主要專注於半導體測試介面之研發設計與製造銷售，目前作為全球最大測試座
（Test Socket）供應商。主要產品涵蓋後端 FT Test、SLT Test、與 Burn-in Test 用之同軸
測試座（Coaxial Socket）、 HyperSocket、Burn-in Socket。今年與 TPI 簽訂五年合作契約
積極擴展前端晶圓測試 CP Test 用之 MEMS Probe Card，並整合主動溫控與液冷技術開
發高工耗測試逤需要的散熱測試解決方案。穎崴目前主要生產基地分布於高雄，分別為
高雄探針廠、高雄仁武廠，仁武廠包含一廠與二廠，其中仁武二廠已於 2Q27 投產。新
竹廠負責探針卡製造。 
2. 1H26 營收結構為 Coaxial Socket 48%、Probe Card 27%、PoP & Plastic Socket 14%、
Contact Element 6%、Others 3%、Burn-in Socket 2%。若按應用別區分，HPC & AI 占比
已達 70%居應用類別首位，其次 Smart Phone 8%、而 Networking／車用及其他合計 
22%。至 3Q26，依 Citi 調研，隨探針卡專案出貨階段性收尾及高階測試座放量，FT 
Test、SLT Test & Burn-in Socket 合計比重回升至 72%。其中 Coaxial Socket 預估 60%、
RF & Plastic Socket 大概 11%、Burn-in Socket 則佔 1%。至於 Probe Card 預估降至 20% 
3. 公司整體合併毛利率於 1Q26 為 43.0%、2Q26 因 MEMS 探針卡占比拉升及新產能折舊影
響降至 38.3%，3Q26 隨測試座比重提高毛利率已回升至 40.5%。各產品線方面，依摩
根大通調研，Socket 與 Contact Elements 毛利率穩定維持在 40%+。而 MEMS Probe Card 
因須外購關鍵探針元件，因此即便 2026 年生效之二期協議已改善獲利，毛利率仍僅約 
30–35%。目前穎崴 Pogo pin 自製程度高達 60%，隨自製彈簧針 pogo pin 月產能由 1H26
的 600 萬 pins 擴至 1H27 的 1,400 萬 pins，將有助進一步支撐 Socket 毛利率。 
4. 客戶比重:截至 2026 年活躍客戶超過 200 家，但營收高度集中於高階 AI、HPC、GPU、
ASIC 與伺服器 CPU 客戶。前十大客戶合計占營收比重達 90%，且 7 奈米及以下先進

### Page 3

製程產品占比高達 89%。根據摩根大通，2026 全年穎崴核心終端客戶營收占比以
Nvidia 居首，佔比大約 50–60%。其次為 AMD，預計 10–15%與 Google 約 2%。
◼ 成長動能：
1. AI GPU／ASIC 推升 SLT 與高階 FT 測試座需求)
AI 晶片朝大封裝、高 Pin 數、高功耗與高速傳輸升級，拉長測試時間並放大 SLT Test 導入
需求，使整體市場進入倍數擴張期。在 TAM 動能上，全球高階 HPC FT Test／SLT Test 測試
座市場規模預計自2026E 的 9 億美元躍升至2028E 的 37 億美元，市場預估從 2026 到 2028
相關產品的市場CAGR 將逾 100%，其中價值量更高的 SLT Test 測試座將由 7 億美元跳升至
31 億美元。隨穎崴切入 Nvidia SLT Test 供應鏈，目前市占率預估由 2026E 的 20% 升至
2028E 的30%，外加 2027 年雲端業者如 Google、AMD 的自研 ASIC 與伺服器 CPU 擴大量產，

FT Test／SLT Test 測試座占穎崴總營收比重預估將於 2027E–2028E 維持於 64%–79% 區
間。根據自有模型估算，測試座業務於 2027E 預計貢獻營收約新台幣 161 億–192 億元、
2028E 擴增至約 274 億–362 億元，整體測試座 2026–2028E 營收 CAGR 區間約為 61%–
89%。
2. HyperSocket 與液冷熱管理產品放量
下一代 AI 晶片正由約 10000 Pin 邁向20000 Pin 以上，且由於 AMD MI455、TPUv9 系列新
世代平台封裝尺寸將跨過 100×100mm 至 120×120mm 門檻，基板翹曲與大電流散熱成為測試瓶
頸。穎崴專利 HyperSocket 結合主動液冷熱管理方案可克服大尺寸封裝翹曲並維持穩定電
性，因此Pogo Pin 單針 ASP 相較既有高階同軸測試座享有約 30%溢價，帶動產品組合持續升
級，目前穎崴公司預估 HyperSocket 與液冷測試解決方案將於 2027 占總營收 5%，但考量客

戶大封裝世代切換節奏，普遍預期放量重心將落在 2028 年。依自有模型的預估，該測試解決
方案預計於2027 貢獻總營收約 3%–5%（約新台幣 9 億–15 億元），2028 有機會放大至約
5%–6%（約新台幣 21 億–32 億元），相較 2027 年呈現倍數級年增動能，並有助高階測試座
在擴產自製化下將 2027E–2028E 合併毛利率支撐於 41.1%–46.2% 區間。

### Page 4

3. 新增產能釋放與 CPO 測試
因應既有產能滿載，高雄仁武二廠已於2Q27 投產，其中新增 Socket 產能自 3 月開出並在第
二季順利認列營收，使 5,000-pin 的等效規格 Socket 月產能自 2026 下半年的 4.2k 套擴增逾
70%至 1H27 的 7.2k 套，自製 Pogo Pin 月產能亦由 900 萬 pins 增逾 50%至 1,400 萬 pins，預
估 2027 年底可達單月 1,600–1,800 萬 pins。新產能開出讓既有高階 AI 積壓的訂單順利轉
化為營收，支撐目前市場與自有模型所預估的穎崴 2027E 總營收區間達新台幣 213.5 億–
303.7 億元（YoY +45%–85%）、2028E 達 351.0 億–528.3 億元（YoY +64%–76%），對應
2026–2028E 總營收 CAGR 區間為 54%–81%（EPS CAGR 區間則達 76%–108%）。CPO 的測試
方案預計2H27 開始小量出貨，營收貢獻大約低個位數以下，至 2028 年進入量產成熟階段，

預計可以貢獻2028 年營收最高至接近 10%。
4. 1H26
1H26 合併營收 65.03 億元，HoH +61.0%、YoY +70.3%（1Q26 為 29.80 億元、2Q26 為
35.23 億元），整體毛利率為 40.5%（1Q26 為 43.0%、2Q26 為 38.3%）。稅後淨利 13.71 億
元（YoY +67.6%、HoH +60.4%；1Q26 為 6.99 億元、2Q26 為 6.72 億元），1H26 累計 EPS
達 38.24 元（1Q26 為 19.54 元、2Q26 為 18.70 元），上半年營收與獲利均創歷年同期新
高。截至最新已公告進度，累計前 9 月合併營收達 110.59 億元（年增 96.7%），同創歷年
同期新高。在產品組合與毛利率比較上，1H26 產品營收比重仍以 Coaxial Socket（48%）與
Probe Card（27%）為核心。
⚫ 與1H25（毛利率 48.9%）比較：1H26 毛利率表現明顯較差（YoY -8.4 個百分點），主

因1H25 銷售較多高毛利的 Non-MEMS Probe Card 及傳統高毛利組合，而 1H26 向
Technoprobe 外購關鍵 MEMS 探針元件的 MEMS Probe Card 營收占比擴大，上述外購元
件結構拉低 MEMS Probe Card 毛利率，使其落在 30%–35%，低於測試座及接觸元件的
40% 以上水準，對整體毛利率形成結構性稀釋。

### Page 5

⚫ 2H26E 穎崴合併營收 79.56 億元（HoH +22.3%、YoY +97.0%），毛利率 41.29%（HoH 
+0.82 ppt、YoY -0.58 ppt），歸屬母公司稅後淨利 17.71 億元（HoH +29.2%、YoY 
+107.1%），基本 EPS 48.76 元（HoH +27.5%、YoY +103.5%）。

⚫ 2026E 穎崴合併營收 144.60 億元（YoY +84.0%），毛利率 40.92%（YoY -4.34 
ppt），歸屬母公司稅後淨利 31.42 億元（YoY +87.8%），基本 EPS 87.04 元（YoY 
+85.5%）。 
5. 2026/2027 
產能與資本支出方面，仁武二廠與下一座新廠預計於 2Q27–1Q28 陸續投產，推升 2027 年探
針月產能達 1,400 萬–1,800 萬支、自製率提高至 50%–60%，5,000-pin 等效測試座月產能於 
1H27 達 7.2k 套等於穎崴整體測試座產能將在 2027 倍增，2027–2028 年度預估的資本支出介
於新台幣 20.0 億–24.0 億元，其中自有模型預估 2027 為 20.0 億到 22.0 億元之間、2028 預估
為 22.0 億到 24.0 億元之間。新產品線方面，受惠高階 Functional Burn-in 升級，2027–2028E 
Burn-in 測試座營收預估為新台幣 6.40 億–10.53 億元，2027 模型預估約 6.40 億元、2028 則
有望到 10.53 億元，占總營收約 3%成為未來關注的成長動能之一。CPO 與矽光子測試預計自 
2H27 起小量出貨，2028E 營收貢獻區間為 5%-10%不等的年營收，端看實際量產與交貨情況。。

## Document 270: An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-26).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

## Document 304: Cliff Notes_ risks mount (2026-11-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ risks mount (2026-11-26).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: risks mount
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:31 November 20 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October, employment falling 43.5k following
September’s 36.4k decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the year have now slowed
to 1.1%yr, materially below current growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the

unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9% despite the participation rate falling
another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

### Page 2

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer momentum in hiring, now we are seeing
evidence of a material increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have remained in
employment to date, but persistent job shedding raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend
in the unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most
certainly before the late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3
increase was skewed towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay outcomes directly
tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements
meanwhile, the most responsive to current and expected labour market conditions, accounted for their
smallest share of a Q3 wage increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour cost

growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident in housing. The Westpac Housing
Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005. Interestingly,
there remains considerable latent demand: almost 13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over
the next year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak sentiment, high interest rates and

expectations of further price declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These factors also
pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for inflation remained the critical theme. The
October FOMC minutes highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs and energy
prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several [members] judg[ing] that price pressures were
increasingly extending beyond industries directly affected by data center construction”. Participants

"assessed that medium- and longer-term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-target inflation is a threat to the status
quo, and perceived risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke throughout the week
also kept the focus on price risks, and called for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again
proposed a 50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at the December and January

meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales
gave further justification for this view this week, the control group surprising to the upside with a 0.6%
gain in October after a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said, housing is under
considerable pressure, pending home sales falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back

at 2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent weakness in this sector will, in time, have
implications for both consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international development of the week. Again, it provided
a call for pro-active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr
and the decline in fixed asset investment broadened outside property, with capital expenditure now

down 8.1% across the economy year-to-date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth; though, even here, momentum softened
in October, annual growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not only activity that

### Page 3

Related articles
remains under pressure, but also wealth, with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective, stimulus needs to affect change in both activity
and wealth and do so in a sustainable manner.

## Document 307: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-27).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

## Document 343: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP.  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

8. Across the English Channel, we expect the MPC to raise Bank Rate by 25bp to 4% on

### Page 6

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP, and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP, although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

## Document 354: Is the economy ‘plucked’_ (2026-11-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Is the economy ‘plucked’_ (2026-11-26).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

A New Keynesian ‘plucking’ model of unemployment and inflation
PRELIMINARY AND INCOMPLETE
Anthony Brassil* and Callum Ryan*
Research Discussion Paper
20XX-YY
December 2025
*Economic Research Department
Reserve Bank of Australia
The views expressed in this paper are those of the authors and should not be attributed to the
Reserve Bank of Australia. Any errors are the sole responsibility of the authors.
Author: ryanca at domain rba.gov.au
External Communications: rbainfo@rba.gov.au

https://doi.org/10.47688/rdp20XX-YY

### Page 2

Abstract
We construct a New Keynesian model with search-and-matching frictions in the labour market that
can generate the nonlinear ‘plucking’ dynamics of unemployment in the data: unemployment tends to
spike upwards and then slowly decline. The model features endogenous layoffs, a ‘slippery’ job ladder,
downward nominal wage rigidity, and convex recruiting costs. It generates both the ‘deepness’ and
‘steepness’ asymmetries in unemployment dynamics, and broadly matches the response of labour-

market flows in a downturn. The model endogeneously generates a Phillips curve relationship that
is strongly nonlinear and state-dependent, and a short-run non-accelerating rate of unemployment
(NAIRU) that fluctuates asymmetrically over the business cycle. We also show that cyclical properties
(e.g. shock volatility, nominal rigidities, policy regime) can have a significant effect on the long-run
NAIRU via agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty.

Keywords:

Figure 1: Unemployment rate in Australia
*Long-run NAIRU estimate from Ballantyne and Cusbert (2025).
1. Introduction
The unemployment rate in the data has strongly nonlinear dynamic (Figure 1). It spikes sharply in
occasional downturns, then declines slowly but persistently in recoveries.1 It is right-skewed in both
levels (i.e. asymmetric ‘deepness’) and in changes (i.e. asymmetric ‘steepness’) (Sichel 1993). Gross
flows between unemployment and employment display similar nonlinearities. Standard search-and-

matching models of the labour market struggle to generate these nonlinearities in unemployment
and labour-market flows (Ferraro 2023).
If this asymmetric pattern stems from frictions within the labour market, what implication do those
frictions have for the relationship between inflation and unemployment? Research using nonlinear
structural models to explore the asymmetric dynamics in the labour market tend to abstract from price
frictions and inflation dynamics (e.g. Dupraz, Nakamura and Steinsson 2025; Ferraro 2018; Petrosky-

Nadeau, Zhang and Kuehn 2018), while in the New Keynesian literature, the usual approach of
linearising equilibrium conditions excludes these nonlinear dynamics by assumption. The 2020s
inflation surge has reignited research into the nonlinearity of the Phillips curve, and the extent to which
this nonlinearity stems from frictions within the labour market (e.g. Benigno and Eggertsson 2023).
But this literature tends not to model the labour market in as much detail.

In this paper, we explore the extent to which a detailed search-and-matching model of the labour
market with pricing frictions can match the nonlinear dynamics in the labour market. We then use
the model to examine the implications of these nonlinearities for the Phillips curve relationship and
the non-accelerating rate of unemployment (NAIRU).
1 Friedman (1964) uses the ‘plucking’ description for the asymmetric way in which output seems to be occasionally ‘plucked’

below trend, before recovering back to trend.

### Page 5

2
We start with a general-equilibrium search-and-matching model similar to Bloesch, Lee and
Weber (2024), in which firms post wages and vacancies and set prices (subject to nominal rigidities
and recruiting costs), and workers search (while unemployed and on the job) and decide whether to
accept and quit jobs. Policy steers aggregate demand to balance inflation and unemployment targets.
We extend this model to include endogenous layoffs, downward nominal wage rigidity, and a ‘slippery’

job ladder (drawing on Dupraz et al (2025)).2 We find that these features - together with convex
recruiting costs - are collectively key to achieving both the asymmetric spikes in unemployment when
shocks occur and the slow speed in the subsequent recovery. The model is sufficiently detailed to
broadly match the dynamics of unemployment and gross flows, but tractable enough that we can
obtain the global nonlinear solution with rational expectations and aggregate uncertainty. This is

important because agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty is a significant determinant
of outcomes, especially the long-run NAIRU.
We show that the model can generate substantial asymmetry in both ‘deepness’ and ‘steepness’
(comparable to or greater than in the data). The response of unemployment, job-finding, and
separations after a stylised negative demand shock broadly matches the response of these variables in
downturns over the past 50 years. Aggregate demand shocks have an especially asymmetric effect,

because the effect on inflation amplifies the effect of the downward nominal wage rigidity. When
supply shocks occur, the inflation response somewhat eases the downward nominal wage constraint.
The model endogenously generates a Phillips curve relationship that is strongly nonlinear and state-
dependent. Conditional on some initial state of the economy, the Phillips curve is much steeper for
low rates of unemployment. When the initial unemployment rate is higher, the Phillips curve shifts to

the right, and when the initial level of real wages is higher, it shifts upwards.
We can also use the model to simulate a ‘short-run NAIRU’, which is the unemployment rate consistent
with eliminating inflationary pressure from the labour market at some cyclical horizon (e.g. 1-2 years).
This short-run NAIRU varies asymmetrically with the state of the economy. It tends to rise when
unemployment is high, but also when unemployment has fallen rapidly (because real wages increase).

Finally, we can use the ‘stochastic steady state’ of the model to explore the determinants of the long-
run NAIRU, i.e. the unemployment rate consistent with stable inflation in the absence of shocks (but
where agents still expect that shocks may occur in the future). We find that when firms account for
the interaction between aggregate risk and asymmetric wage adjustment costs in their wage-setting
decisions, it generates a trade-off between unemployment variance and steady-state unemployment.

For example, if policy places greater weight on reducing the gap between unemployment and the
long-run NAIRU (instead of the gap between inflation and target), it tends to increase the long-run
NAIRU. This is because by mitigating fluctuations in labour demand, policy reduces the risk of hitting
downward nominal wage rigidities. Firms are then more willing to set higher real wages in steady
state, which reduces the optimal amount of employment in steady state.

The remainder of this introduction discusses the literature. Section 2 outlines the model. Section 3
evaluates the extent to which it can match the nonlinear dynamics in labour-market data. Section 4
considers the implications for the Phillips curve and NAIRU.
2 By ‘slippery’ job ladder, we mean that workers recently hired out of unemployed are much more likely to be laid off.

### Page 6

3
Literature review. The cyclical asymmetries in unemployment are established empirically by e.g.
Sichel (1993), McKay and Reis (2008), and Dupraz et al (2025). Ferraro (2023) analyses the ability of
the baseline Diamond-Mortensen-Pissarides (DMP) model to produce these asymmetries, and finds
that it cannot generate the ‘steepness’ asymmetry (i.e. right-skewness in unemployment changes).
Petrosky-Nadeau et al (2018) finds that the DMP model can generate the ‘deepness’ asymmetry given

large enough shocks.
Dupraz et al (2025) and Ferraro (2018) propose search-and-matching models that can generate
sharp spikes and slow declines in unemployment. We take several features from Dupraz et al (2025),
including downward nominal wage rigidity and a ‘slippery’ job ladder. Pizzinelli, Theodoridis and
Zanetti (2020) explore the state-dependence of unemployment fluctuations in a model with
endogenous layoffs and on-the-job search.3 Adjemian, Karamé and Langot (2021) estimate a

nonlinear model with endogenous separations, downward wage rigidity, worker heterogeneity and
segmented markets, and show that it can generate similar asymmetries in unemployment, hiring,
and separations to the data.
Our definition of a ‘short-run NAIRU’ is similar to the concept discussed by Hall and Kudlyak (n.d.), who
argue that the natural rate of unemployment may vary cyclically and track the actual unemployment
rate during recoveries. Hall and Kudlyak (2022) discusses the ability of DMP-style models to generate

the slow persistent recovery in unemployment that occurs after recessions. Fujita and Ramey (2007)
show that adding vacancy adjustment costs to a DMP model can produce a more sluggish response
for vacancies and hiring after a downturn, slowing down the recovery in unemployment. Mercan,
Schoefer and Sedláček (2024) suggest a congestion mechanism, whereby new hires and incumbent
workers are imperfect substitutes in production, which also propagates unemployment after a shock.

This literature above generally abstracts from price rigidities and inflation dynamics. There is a
substantial literature incorporating labour-market frictions into New Keynesian models (see e.g.
Blanchard and Galí 2010). Recent examples include Moscarini and Postel-Vinay (2023), Faccini and
Melosi (2025), and Bloesch et al (2024). These papers focus on the role of on-the-job search and wage
competition between firms in inflation dynamics. We adopt a similar model for wage setting, drawing

on the tractable formulation in Bloesch et al (2024). But these papers use linear approximations.
The literature on downward nominal wage rigidity explores the extent to which it generates a
nonlinear Phillips curve and asymmetric cyclical dynamics (see e.g. Kim and Ruge-Murcia 2009;
Abbritti and Fahr 2013; Daly and Hobijn 2014; Benigno and Antonio Ricci 2011; Schmitt-Grohé
and Uribe 2022; Benigno and Eggertsson 2023; Benigno and Eggertsson 2024). Lepetit (2020)

also constructs a New Keynesian model with asymmetric unemployment dynamics, although the
asymmetry stems from the convex relationship between the job-finding rate and unemployment.
This literature does not model the labour market in as much detail (e.g. endogenous layoffs, on-the-
job search, job ladder) and does not seek to match the ‘plucking’ property in unemployment to the
same extent.
3 Fujita and Ramey (2012) also analyse versions of the DMP model with endogenous separations and on-the-job search,

but their focus is not on asymmetries in unemployment rate dynamics

### Page 7

4
2. Model
The model consists of firms, workers, and a policy rule. We start by outlining the firms’ problem
and optimal choices. Firms’ optimal decision-making drives most of the model’s mechanics. We then
discuss workers and the policy regime, which are modelled more simply. 5 contains a full list of
equilibrium conditions, as well as details of the calibration and solution method.
2.1 Firms
Firms make decisions around hiring, separations, and price- and wage-setting.

Hiring: Firms choose how many vacancies to post, V i t . These vacancies generate Mi t = qtV i t matches,
where qt is determined by a matching function and depends on aggregate matching efficiency and
labour market tightness. A proportion 1 − ϕ E t of these matches are unemployed searchers (where ϕ E t
is the share of unemployed searchers in total searchers, weighted by search intensity).
Firms must screen matches. Then the individual productivities of employed matches are revealed.

Firms make offers to those with productivities above some threshold xi t , which is 1−F i t of them (where
F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual productivity distribution). For unemployed matches,
firms have no information about their individual productivity and make offers to all of them. This
feature proxies for the idea that firms may be more uncertain about the capabilities of unemployed
applicants.
Matches with offers choose whether to accept them; Ace t (W i t ) and employed matches and Acu t (W i t )

of unemployed matches do so, where W i t is the real wage paid by the firm. These job-acceptance
probabilities are characterised in the description of workers below.
The number of new hires is given by
Hi t = ϕ E t Mi t (1 − F i t )Ace t (W i t ) + (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ).
Define Hi,u t = (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ) as the number of hires from unemployment.
Recruiting costs include a per-vacancy cost and a per-match ‘screening’ cost:

Separations: There are two types of separation: quits and layoffs. Each period, Qt (W i t ) of firms’
existing employees quit (either to unemployment or to another job). Just as firms’ hiring rate is

increasing in the real wage they offer (via the job-acceptance probabilities Ace t (W i t ) and Acu t (W i t )),
their quits rate is decreasing in the real wage by the firm. This function is characterised in the section
below on workers.
Workers’ probability of being laid off depends on whether they are insecurely or securely attached.
Insecurely attached employees are all those who have just been hired out of unemployed in
the previous period. Before aggregate shocks are revealed, some portion of new hires from

unemployment, δ u, (i.e. who were hired the previous period), draw a new productivity from

employees who are not laid off then because securely attached (together with all those who have
been employed for more than one period).
After aggregate shocks are revealed and quitting decisions made, δ of securely attached employees

draw a new individual productivity. Firms then decide to lay off those whose productivities are less
than some threshold xi t , which is F i t of them (where F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual
productivity distribution). The layoff probability for securely attached employees will be lower than
for insecurely attached employees both because δ < δ u and the distribution F has less mass at low
values than G.

2.2 Workers
All workers who are unemployed when shocks are revealed search for jobs. A share λ EE of employed
workers also search. The total number of searchers is then λ EE ˜Nt−1 + 1 − ˜Nt−1, and the effective

Workers’ only role in the model is to determine the labour supply schedule faced by firms. They do so
by determining the hiring and quits elasticities ε He t , ε Hu t and ε Q t via the functions Ace t (Wt ), Acu t (Wt )
and Q(Wt ).
Workers draw an idiosyncratic preferences for firms when they match, which they compare to any
wage differential. These preferences are drawn from a Gumbel distribution with parameter γ. If a
firm pays wage Wt , then the probability that a worker prefers a different firm that has made them an

offer is
